Full discussion of the draft law amending Law 4/2007 of 20 April 2007 on the coordination of local police
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u2	This bill has been amended to the full extent of the return, in particular the amendment of the Joint Parliamentary Group and an amendment of the Parliamentary Group on the Glaga Alternative from the Left. Mr. Alfonso Rueda Valenzuela has the floor for the introduction of the bill.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u3	Good morning, Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen. I am pleased today to present the draft amendment to Law 4/2007, coordination of the local police in Galicia. First of all, I would like to thank the agreement of the majority of Members of this House on the occasion of this legal reform. It is true that there are two amendments to return, but it is also true that 85% of Members and Members of this House have shown themselves in favour of this legal reform, which I think is a good idea of its need. So the first one I also want to highlight is the dialogueal character that has been observed by the Galician Union since the very first moment of the drafting of this bill. A technical working group was set up within the Local Police Coordination Commission; four police unions participated; the Local Police Chiefs and Commands Association participated; four municipalities also participated in different Greek political signs. This working group held several meetings, long in addition, in which they were moving forward in a text that, in addition, union representatives, as progress and consensus were made, circulated among their affiliates to receive suggestions. So the result of this work is a legal modification project that has two main features. First, it is necessary, it was necessary; and second, it is necessarily also limited, since it is included, and only that, which was the object of express request by the affected, that is, the local police professionals themselves and the municipalities. The text that is being discussed today therefore has a first primary objective, which is to solve a serious problem arising from the inadequacy of the law adopted in 2007, the coordination of local police, the criteria for equal treatment in employment that had marked a European Union directive as early as 2000. In November 2014, a year and a half ago, the Court of Justice of the European Union issued a judgment in which it considered discriminatory an age limit establishing the local police coordination law in Asturias, set at 30 years, the maximum limit for access to the bodies of the local Asturias Police. The Greek law, which was of the same year as the Asturian law of 2007, also imposed – well it is true that a little higher, 36 years – a similar limit to participate in the oppositions. And, therefore, consistent with this statement made by the European Court of Justice, the Supreme Court of Justice of Galicia also ruled in 2014 against the bases of the provision of squares of a Greek municipality, the Baiona Council – there were many more with this limit, according to the law – and declared null and void those bases for imposing that age limit. There was also another transcendental problem that was limiting the call for squares and that judicial control was also in the law of 2007, and the High Court of Justice established that it was not legal to exempt the requirement of titration from those aspirants who passed a specific course at the Galician Academy of Public Safety, that is, the so-called dispensation of degree. This was also in the law of 2007, and this was also declared legal, and it was therefore necessary to reform it through the reform of the law. The result has already been said to have been a paralyse since the year 2014 of the convening of squares for local police bodies. The municipalities were in a difficult situation of exit and, in any case, of legal insecurity. If they called according to the law of 2007, their bases could be traced, and if they didn't call according to the law of the year 2007, too; until the law was changed, their bases could be traced. Therefore, this change of law was essential, which was as much as unblocking this situation and adapting our law to judicial pronouncements. So I say that the fundamental objective of today's law is to allow the Greek Councils to cover the necessary squares to maintain their local police services with the appropriate templates and, above all, with the necessary legal certainty in the calls they decide to make. But also, by taking advantage of this change and collecting the suggestions – I insist – of those directly affected, in this law it is taken advantage of to introduce other changes, other novelties that have been requested. For example, when it comes to internal advocacy calls – they were also stuck in many municipalities – and therefore what we do is to simplify the mechanism, and now, when it comes to the possibility of having to cover a square by internal promotion, the councils have no other remedy than to capture their new internal advocacy staff between two groups: on the one hand, the local police personnel of that municipality itself, who have to be in the category immediately below and with three years of age, or otherwise, as an alternative, may come to the local police of other municipalities but have to be in the category of the plaza they want to cover. Therefore, this system caused many difficulties in covering the squares, in practice necessary squares, and therefore we opened up the possibility of personal capture through the way of internal promotion in two other groups: on the one hand, the staff of the same municipality but of two lower categories, not only one, and on the other hand, the staff of other municipalities of an immediately lower category, well it is true that it is as a subsidiary alternative; first, it goes to the traditional system, but, if it was not possible – and in many cases it was not being possible – to open the two pathways. Also, to facilitate the coverage of squares, we introduced changes in the regulation of permutations between agents who want an exchange being different municipalities. So the requirement that there was until now that it would prevent changes if the agents concerned were less than five years old to move on to the second activity situation because of age. He had no reason to be. We have all come to this conclusion, to introduce this requirement, and therefore it has been considered necessary to maintain this veto, and it is no longer necessary to have that limit of five years before the retirement age. We also introduced flexibility in the first activity. Until now, the municipalities have been allowed to extend the passage annually to the second activity, but a favourable medical report is required annually issued by a medical tribunal for this purpose. This is to no one escape that it was a far-sighted requirement that put many impediments in practice, bureaucratic impediments; many times that simply the professionals decided not to opt for this system, it was a more annual renewal process. It is now replaced by the need to set up ad hoc ad hoc medical tribunal by a favourable medical report, which may be issued by the head doctor. And finally, the criteria for forcing agents into forced retirement are also used. In 2007 all permanent disability was excluded from the second activity and was therefore referred – there was no other remedy – to compulsory retirement. In this bill, this obligation is satisfied and it is established that retirement will be compulsory only in the case of absolute permanent disabilities. We believe that another case of permanent disabilities can prevent the police and the local police from performing specific police duties, at least not doing all the tasks, but rather more administrative, more auxiliary, than forced retirement. So these changes have a range that we believe is very important for the day-to-day operation of the police. We solve urgent problems and, above all, situations and problems that required a solution by the municipalities and by the professionals, and therefore we make these bodies more flexible and effective to the thousands of professionals who make up these bodies at the local police in Galicia. I would also like, to complete the explanation of this law, to refer to three other amendments which are more accessory but which correspond to the necessary adaptation that we had to make, especially by taking advantage of the amendment, since the law of 2007, apart from those declared illegal precepts, had need to change after ten years of its entry into force and after other laws had come into force that required this amendment. In these years, an organic law of the disciplinary regime of National Police has been passed, with provision for application to local police, and therefore the disciplinary regime provided for in the law of 2007 for local police in Galicia has to be adapted to this national framework adopted subsequently in this organic law. Also the quarterly meetings of the Local Police Coordination Commission had no reason to be, with which they were of a semi-annual nature, was sufficient, and therefore this amendment was made. And we must also not forget – very important – that it was approved in Galicia, in the State first, the Basic Statute of the Public Employee, then in Galicia in its development the Public Employment Act of Galicia, and therefore needed to make necessary adaptations also in local police bodies, setting out minimum contents that should be published on the basis of calls to promote equal treatment of all competitors, preventing, as it does in the courts of selective processes in Galicia, participating in local police selection courts people who in the previous five years had to perform tasks of preparing applicants or of collaboration with academies. I'm saying, in general, small changes that come from the Public Employment Act and that it was necessary to transpose into the local police bodies as well. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we have developed a normative amendment – I say – with the consensus and momentum of all those involved, and which therefore responds to their main demands: of legal security, of elimination of discrimination, which the courts of justice have declared arbitrary, with greater rigour in the procedures of free access, with greater flexibility in the process of provision of squares for internal promotion, with greater possibilities of mobility and with more ease for the active stay of those staff who wish to do so. The text – I insist – was drafted by a working group, by representatives of all the groups present in the Local Police Coordination and Referenda Commission – I believe this is also important to make it clear – by the unanimity of this Local Police Coordination Commission, once the working group has given it the final drafting. I sincerely believe that this change that we are now presenting is consistent with the impulse that has been giving the Galician Union in recent years to the local police corps. Nothing else entered this government, in 2009, we were transferred to the demand to homogenize the image of local police bodies throughout the country. We believe it was very important that they were recognized as one body throughout Galicia, regardless of their membership in different municipalities. And the decree of uniformity was passed, which made the models of wardrobes, badges, meats and labeling vehicles. It's published. And we've also been asked to improve our training. We passed from 86 courses, with 4,172 squares, in 2009, to almost 200, with 5,500 squares, last year 2015. We also approve common protocols of action, which are ending their development, will be as late as the beginning of the next semester. It must also be said that all training courses are being developed on the basis of requests that local police professionals themselves make to us on a web platform, a web survey, which is designed for that. And we're placing particular emphasis on local police's action in the fight against gender-based violence. We want to involve them directly in the early detection of gender-based violence, and therefore, from there, that explains to a large extent the impulse to the constitution of local tables of gender-based violence, in which, in all cases, when there is a local police force, they have a very important participation. We were also asked for more means for local police bodies, and we must say that we made an important effort in this, and this last year 55 cars and 26 motorcycles were handed over to the local police bodies in Galicia, subsidized to 100 percent by the Galician Union. And we believe that, especially for the small municipalities, it is a very important support for the development of the work of their police. We have also provided the municipalities around a thousand Tetra terminals, which is as much as saying that we can incorporate Galician emergency digital network, improving in all cases – I think to a large extent – the quality of communications that up to now had the local Galician police. And so, with all this, we've contributed to better equipped, better organized, better functioning local police bodies. I think something will have to do with Galicia being the fourth most secure autonomous community in the whole state of Spain. I think they have a lot to do with the professionalism, the preparation and equipment of the local police bodies. I say: how the local police and police in Galicia are the ones who know their needs best, who told us what needed to be modified in this law, who worked, and I take advantage of this attendance to thank them all those meetings, all that time they devoted, not only to the political representatives of these municipalities, not only to the Fegamp, but especially to the local police, to the police unions and professionals who did these jobs. Your opinion is what we bring to this House today, such as the Bill of Amendment of the Law of the Year 2007. And therefore, just as we have the consensus of these professionals, I hope that we will have the consensus of this House to improve, as I say, legal security, flexibility, mobility and, ultimately, the security of all the Greeks through the thousands of professionals who work as local police around Galicia every day. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u4	Thank you, Vice President. Round of defense of amendments to the whole. For the Joint Parliamentary Group, Mrs. Fernández Rodríguez.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u5	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. This bill only updates the existing legislation, aspects of Law 4/2007, of 20 April, of coordination of local police. It is a further example of how the Government of Feijóo leaves the transfer of statutory competences to die by inannition, in this case the competences of citizen security. In this context of annihilation, the Government of the Union is bound to follow, with reforms in the aspects affected by state legislation and by the Supreme Court's judgement on the limitation of age for access to the local police, which was in 36 years. Other issues that need updates and adjustments, even substantial advances, should also appear at a time of crisis that requires them. Even so, this bill aims to be electoralist, not responding to the needs of the local police working environment and the provision of services in the municipalities, which are the administrations closest to citizenship. Nowhere does a shock plan arrive to change the current situation of the local police staff in Galicia, which is 40% lower than that of the United States and the European Union: vacancies, surpluses, municipalities that resort to municipal monitors to perform local police functions, templates with less than 3 officers, unmet night services, lack of personnel to perform each service among the staff. The big deficit in Galicia doesn't understand cities, towns and small towns. The crisis has aggravated the deficiencies of the police units in the 129 Greek municipalities that currently have municipal agents, to the point of having more than 10% unmet squares. It's an average of 1.8 vacancies per 1,000 inhabitants. If we comply with the Local Police Coordination Act of 2007, which we are reforming, of the 2,500 places that are now, we should reach more than 4,200 agents. In conclusion, 1 out of every 5 municipalities violates the law of the Union, which is still absent, and appears just before the elections to Parliament, to make small reforms. Not to mention the municipalities, where there's no one waiting for the local police force. There is a very critical situation in 10 municipalities, where there is only one police officer, who is responsible for the security of the citizen throughout his county, without the possibility of taking turns or sharing vacations or reconciling his own family life. We're facing an economic problem, because many municipalities can't support the local police force, and make it impossible to cover the casualties, retirement vacancies and transfers themselves. Where's the government of Feijóo to solve these bees? It's not even expected. But in addition, the central government decided to freeze the public administration's staffing, prohibiting the filling of vacant squares, except health personnel and teaching staff, where a 10% replacement rate was not allowed. He then agreed to and accepted an allegation from the police organizations that the state's security forces and bodies would also be included among the exceptions. Some small or medium-sized council didn't even cover that 10% allowed for lack of resources, or because 10% of 1, 2, 3 and even 10 squares can't afford to convene a vacant square. They legislate to great, and then come the consequences, indescriptible, because precisely in these municipalities is where the citizen's worst security coverage is, after years of crisis, and then what happens next. At the end of 2014, the Council of Ministers agreed to increase from 10% to 100% the rate of replacement of troops in local police bodies for the municipalities that had met their objectives of budgetary stability and public debt. We have tabled a complete amendment to this project, requesting its return to the Government. This change is minimalist, in addition to being a clear example of legislate, without responding to real problems. With total obvious evidence, normative advances related to the protection of maternity. At least it would be desirable to enable the Government to incorporate into the regulatory development of the draft law protection rules for the situation of pregnancy, childbirth or puerpery, especially in the tests of access to local police squares. This does appear to be collected in the regulation of the oppositions of the National Police, where you can look at the model and even improve. If the aspirants cannot perform the physical tests for pregnancy or childbirth, they will do all the rest, conditioning their entry to the body in the overcoming of those who, according to their overall qualification in the summons in relation to the other aspirants, correspond to them. The person concerned could perform the specified physical tests at the time of the next call, and, if on that date she could not die from another properly accredited pregnancy or delivery, she could perform them at the same time as an immediate second call. Incorporation could be admitted to or admitted to that summons in which it has passed physical evidence. We cannot fail to demand an article dedicated specifically to the protection of trade union action and collective bargaining in the local police force, according to the basic Statute of the employee and the public employee, because the local police is a personal official, and we refer ourselves to article 15 of the individual rights exercised collectively, whether it is union freedom and collective bargaining and participation in the determination of working conditions. In general, we find ourselves on this subject in the face of a chronic ejaculation in the normative changes promoted by the People's Party, which are undermining or obviating the regulation that must protect the right to collective bargaining and union representation, which we understand is fundamental to defending the rights of workers and workers. Another section of great weight affects the fact that the change overlooks the need for the municipalities to collaborate to provide the local police services grouped. It does not seem realistic to rule out legislative progress, when the most recent rules cover ways of collaboration that have been opened from the amendment of the Law of Local Regime bases, which has broadened the possibility, in accordance with the additional provision of the Organic Law of Forces and Security Bodies, which was regulated by Order 2944/2010 of 10 November, so that the conditions for the association of municipalities are determined, in order to provide local police services. We also believe that the articulate should define both the requirements and the procedure for intermunicipal collaboration, but as the Government of Beijóo has marked itself as objective to manage less than it corresponds to the Statute, so we get the hair. You're not going to think it's impossible, look at Catalonia, Basque Country, or even Madrid, which has already established a broad regulation in 1991 to ensure the unity of local police services within the framework of their competences. Two or more neighbouring municipalities, belonging to the same autonomous community, which do not have sufficient resources separately for the provision of local police services, can be associated with the execution of functions assigned to local police, in accordance with the additional fifth provision of Organic Law 2/1986 of 13 March, of forces and security bodies. Currently, the so-called inter-municipal collaboration agreement launched by the Ministry of the Interior, endorsed and disseminated to the municipalities by the Union and the Galega Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, allows collaborations between local police, but is limited to predictable events, such as the celebration of employers' parties. In the face of this ban, even representatives such as Axempol, the Association of Chiefs and Commands of the Local Police of Galicia, claim to go further, and claim that this collaboration, which is currently stagnated, since the State commission which was created to drive it is paralysed, if allowed in alleged emergencies, in severe traffic accidents, security or traffic controls, which require special instrumentalization or personal qualification, or in administrative inspections requiring special qualification... Even police commanders expected that the proposal that was processed by the Directorate General for Emergency and Internal Affairs would be collected in the reform of this law. Why not combine the local police public service between several municipalities? The Government of Feijóo can't do anything, because it hasn't claimed the competences corresponding to the Central Government in the years of the presidency of the Union. In addition, within this amendment, of the Local Police Coordination Act, we need to add the amendment of articles which do not appear to be amended, and we believe that it is very important, within the context of Article 9 of the Law of 2007, uniformity, because all members of the Local Police must have PPEs which are relevant to the prevention of occupational risks, in particular anti-ball vests. We also understand that it is very important that Article 11 should be modified, which is what is meant by technical means, which are elements, devices and systems of police bodies that use to comply with their obligations, because we understand that, in order to ensure their continuing and practical formation, the councils should jointly have a shooting gallery, because unfortunately, the reality is that there are police officers who have no weapon, when it is mandatory. Justifying to have a weapon means having a gunman, a shooting gallery and a whole logistics that can't be allowed. And this is a great ejacist in many municipalities, not only where there's only one local cop, but where there's even a significant number of them. We also understand very important that the oral and written knowledge of our language should be expounded, as determined by the legislation itself. We also understand very important – something we have already talked about – access to local police tests and examinations on the topic of pregnant women. This is in relation to the differentiation of the various groups that make up this body, which, in the case of pregnancy of local police officers or police officers of the municipalities which have not constituted the local police force, will apply to them the provisions of Law 31/95 of 8 November on the prevention of occupational risks in matters relating to the protection of maternity. This law collects the assessment of the risks of pregnant workers or recent delivery to agents, procedures or working conditions that may negatively influence the health of workers themselves or the fetus, in any activity likely to present a specific risk, as there is still a great risk in their own adaptation to work. So we understand that there are certain kinds of jobs, such as night work and shift work, which can be dangerous. And even the fact that you can get away from work with the right to pay for prenatal examinations and your own techniques, even if there isn't a proper job to your condition, to this state, a temporary discharge or suspension. We also believe that Article 15 should be amended with regard to the establishment of the working hours, rests and other working conditions, which will be the subject of negotiations with trade union representatives in accordance with the terms laid down by the legislation in force, since in the amendment itself there is nothing of the union's own work. We also understand that it is very important – and we stress – the fact that all the local police officers in Galicia must be trained in gender equality and in prevention and fight against it, because we understand that many courses are missing. In addition, in all local police bodies in Galicia there will be a group of agents with a positive assessment of gender-specific training and awareness-raising and gender-based violence that will meet the demands generated in relation to the prevention and protection of victims, ensuring the service during the 24 hours of the 365 days of the year. In order to ensure the provision of these services, the councils may proceed to sign a partnership agreement, so that they can provide a joint service which guarantees the best protection for victims. This will appear reflected in Law No. 11/2007 of 27 July 2007 in the Welsh Law for the Prevention and Comprehensive Treatment of Gender Violence, in which it spoke of the prevention of gender-based violence, which were measures to raise awareness of gender-based violence, and what was the very design of campaign development and dissemination. We understand that the Galician Union itself would have to make the necessary arrangements with other public administrations to ensure the dissemination of awareness campaigns in the centres dependent on local administration, the administrations of justice and between the state and local police security forces and bodies. We also believe that a review of sanctions should be carried out so that, within the date of entry into force of this law – of its own – the application itself is in fact more favourable for the person to function sanctioned. Resolutions will be reviewed on their own, even if they are firm, whose compliance does not begin or end on the date ahead. Revision of resolutions imposing a separation penalty on the service will not be possible. Or the fact of the misalignment of the weapon itself, which we understand is too serious. Thank you very much.

And from that approach, it's how we understand that this reform of the law is necessary, yes, but it's late, in an electoral way and in an epidermal way. We understand that reform must provide the legal mechanisms that prevent the caciquil use of local police. As for evas, we can list some of them, for example, there is a need to establish an effective mechanism for monitoring and observing the strict compliance with the terms set out in the law to give the function of coordination and unification. And this goes precisely to one of the eves of what it represents, because the Coordinating Committee, which in the current text of the law has four meetings, has two. I mean, it's like making it clear that it's not done considering the current reality of the local police. That Coordinating Commission through four meetings was not able to coordinate, and you want it to be done through two. So we are very afraid that the only thing that this Coordinating Commission does is that it will be a body that will meet twice, but without the firm decision to enforce what is stated in the law. And we're very afraid they'll work on that line, because, from four to two, of course, it gets worse. The need to take into account the plurality existing in local police bodies is a demand for local police unions, which say that representativeness must be in agreement with it, that is, to reality, and that it allows real participation, that allows dialogue, so that decisions that benefit the local police body best, and therefore the local police public service, and therefore the Greek citizenship. The current Coordinating Commission does not seem to respond to that plural reality. And the reality itself is seen in the numbers that are indicated. We can't afford to pass the occasion of regulating this aspect, because we will avoid the endogenous loopholes that only provide the service according to certain power quotas. Because the local police is also seen as a form of power, power quotas, and that's what the law has to prevent, and put all possible remedies to have a public service according to the values of democracy, the values of ethics, and to prevent the cacical use of this body. The professionalization of services and the quality of public service cannot be subject to the wishes of anyone, regardless of the sign. Citizenship, wherever it is, lives where it lives, deserves the same quality public service with regard to this body. This logically leads to the fact that, in some cases, this wage differentiation can reach an average of EUR 800 per month, being identical to antiquity, services and functions. So that makes there a logical desire to move from one municipality to another, depending precisely on that wage difference. I mean, we think it's necessary to act there to homogenize what that function is, and also to take into account the requirements of service and function antiquity. In Decree 243/2008 of 16 October 2007 and Law 4/2007 of 20 April 2007 was established in its exhibition part – and something has already been said by the Joint Group – that it is advisable to aspire to 1.8 policemen per 1,000 inhabitants. It is clear that this is shocking to Article 30.1 of Law 4/2007, which states that the minimum number must be 2 police officers and 1 officer. This, of course, contrasts with the legislation at other points in the Spanish State, for example, the bat, where it is established that the number will be 6 police, 1 cable and a sergeant; or with the recent draft law presented in Andalusia, which indicates a minimum of 5 members in Article 12.1, or in the localities of more than 5,000 inhabitants. In Galicia, there are situations that, in addition, violate that safety precept that you so much want, giving anomalous situations in municipalities with only 1 police or 2; in municipalities of 10,000 people with 10 police; and in large cities – we know – that they all have staffing deficits, all of them. The ideal must be a even number; this is a complaint above all to avoid the danger of a person's round, it's obvious. It is therefore necessary to regulate patrol two, and thus to avoid risk situations for the local police in cases where the individual street tour is permitted. None of the 129 municipalities with local police meet the ratios. It is clear to regulate horizontal mobility between localities and vertical within the locality in professional promotion, avoiding the endogenous "sound course" so that the constitutional principles of merit, capacity, equality and publicity are logical and not anomaly, as well as a process that we understand should be centralized and that it should be the best indication that we move forward in the professionalization of this public service. Finally, another of the demands is that for a better development of the legislation it is necessary to lay down organic regulations for the operation of the local police public service. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u38	Thank you, President. Good morning to all and to all. Well, this bill aims to regulate the double function of the board of lawyers of the Board: judicial counseling and defence in judgment of the Self-Government. Let's keep some of the amendments we tabled. In particular, Amendment 1 to Article 11 because we believe that it is in clear incongruence between the regulation in paragraph 3 and paragraph 5. Paragraph 3 states that it is not necessary to act by means of a prosecutor. However, paragraph 5 sets out the possibility for the Board to include in its fee-taxation the costs corresponding to the search functions. Which is why the two are contradictory, if you do not need a proxy, the search rates should not appear. We believe that the agreed transaction of Article 20(2) with regard to the designation of counsel or lawyer and the waiver of the defence of counsel from Union, guarantees the right of public workers and workers to the free choice of lawyer or lawyer. We maintain Amendments Nos 3 and 4 to Article 21(2), because we believe that once legal services have been granted authorization, it should not be possible to revoke this concession, as it may create defence problems. At least a period should be set within which such a concession may be revoked so as not to leave the affected worker or worker in defence. We also maintain Amendment No 5, which eliminates Article 22(3), which establishes administrative silence with a negative meaning. We understand that administrative silence should have a positive meaning, and the reasoned resolution of any denial of legal aid should be binding. We understand that it is not logical and that it is neither transparent nor fair that someone is denied the legal assistance of the Union without a justification for such denial. We therefore believe that it creates defence among workers and public workers. We also maintain Amendment No 6, to delete Article 23, which establishes the obligation of the staff to the service of the Administration to bear all representation costs other than defence constraints. It doesn't seem logical that staff should bear expenses arising from the exercise of their function or office, or from the fulfilment of orders from their superiors, and therefore according to this you have to assume costs. The costs must be assumed by the Administration itself, since it is the entity to which it belongs, like any company, in the face of a lack of its employees. They always have the responsibility to bear those costs. These costs should therefore be assumed by the Administration, as long as it is exempt from the payment of fees and deposits and when it has its own means of performing the expert tests. Therefore, public workers and workers are being forced to assume a cost that would not exist for Galician Union itself. We maintain Amendment No 7 to Article 24(2), which designates the person who holds the General Directorate of the General Legal Counsel to decide on the assistance, defence and representation of the persons concerned in the event that a proceeding affects several persons entitled to defence and conflicting interests. No rules or rules are laid down on how to solve this problem, so the decision remains to the free will of those with the General Directorate. It doesn't seem to be the measure or the fairest way to make this decision, much less when it is not collected – as we said before – the obligation to justify why your request was rejected. We also maintain Amendment No 9, to delete Article 25, since we believe that once the authorization for the use of legal services is granted, it is not ethical to begin the process with a person and in the development of that person to abandon it. We believe that the transaction of Amendments Nos 10 and 11 of the Joint Group to Article 26 regulates the assumption that the costs to which the opposite party is convicted are limited by judicial decision and do not cover all the costs of assistance and representation incurred by the public employee when introducing that the Administration will bear the costs that are not covered by the opposite party. At the same time, it guarantees the return by the Administration of expenses relating to the establishment of bank avalanche for the guarantee of defence expenses in the alleged denial of the authorization for the existence of conflicting interests, as regulated in paragraph 2 of the same article, when the judgment is favourable to the public worker in litigation. That is, if you deny the legal assistance of Joina to a worker or a public worker and after that trial you are recognized for your reason and, therefore, convicted of the opposite side, the Board undertakes to return all the costs it had for the worker, including the bank avalanche, through this transaction. We maintain Amendments Nos 13 and 14 of Chapters IV and V respectively, because they regulate matters which under the Basic Statute of the Public Employee must be negotiated with the legal representation of staff in the service of the Greek Administration, such as the relationship of jobs, functions and the provision of posts. Something insistent in the People's Party bills, something insistent in the People's Party bill proposals is the elimination of the obligation to negotiate with the legal representation of workers and workers. They're not eevas, it's a way of understanding politics, it's a way of understanding working relationships, and it's a way of understanding the role of union representation of workers and public workers. Of course, it's a board that denies the right to collective bargaining of public workers, but what it can't also refuse to negotiate and deal with the legal representation of public workers and workers such as the RPT, the functions or the provision of jobs. It's a serious breach, a repeated breach. That's why it's worth voting against this law, but we're not going to do it because rights are collected because of other rules, even if this obligation to negotiate is not included here. We maintain Amendment No 15 to paragraph 2 of the first final provision, which provides that the Council of Galicia will approve the organizational structure of the General Legal Counsel, thus determining its dependent organs, its powers and functions, without regard to the objective negotiation with the legal representation of public workers, as set out in Article 37 of the Basic Statute of the Public Employee, which considers them matters of collective bargaining. Article 37.1 of the Basic Statute of the Public Employee states: "They shall be negotiated in their respective fields in relation to the competences of each Public Administration and to the extent legally applicable in each case, the following matters..." and in paragraph (c)di: "The rules establishing general criteria for access, career, provision, job classification systems, and plans and human resources planning instruments". We therefore believe that this text does not comply with what is set out in the Basic Statute. In the same vein, Article 13.2 of Law 55/2003 of 16 December 2003 of the Framework Staff Regulations for the Staff Regulations of Health Services states: "Human resources management plans shall be approved, published or, where appropriate, notified, in the manner in which each health service is determined. They will be previously negotiated on the corresponding tables." In other words, both the official staff and the staff, as well as the statutory staff, in the three cases the Board's obligation to negotiate these conditions of access provision and the TPPs with the legal representation of workers and workers. I think we're still in time for the Board to recapitate and include that obligation, since just what it lets see is that it won't prevent that negotiation but it's within its philosophy and its way of understanding the labor relations and the role of trade union organizations is not in line with our democracy. Although he is a child and poor, the position the People's Party takes in this regard is even more anti-democratic, it does not correspond to our democratic state. It is therefore clear that the obligation to negotiate prior to the change in the powers and powers of the staff assigned to the General Directorate of the General Legal Counsel, as well as the structure of it, is a requirement which has been obvious in the present case. So, and even considering that this bill has a lot of eves, which is much better, but also in the hope that within a few months a new Government can improve this bill, we will proceed to abstain in the vote. Nothing else, thank you very much.

The original text sent to us by the Government, after making the references to all the statutory and regulatory provisions binding on the use of the Welsh language, says that: "It will be thought that yours..." – i.e. in the field of general legal advice – "...actuations, especially those addressed to other organs of the autonomous administration and those taking place in front of the judicial organs rooted in Galician territory, are usually done in Greek." I mean, it's an absolutely lax wording, absolutely imperative, and in which words are used so little coercive from a legal point of view as "care" and "usually." Finally, there has been a transaction to this original text in which also, by keeping the first heading, the following wording is maintained: "You will be careful that all your actions, in particular those addressed to other organs of the Autonomous Administration and those produced in front of the judicial organs rooted in the Greek territory, are done in Greek." We, for our parliamentary group, although, indeed, there is a minimum advance in respect of the original text in the sense that it is removed from the "normally" to introduce the "all its actions", refers clearly to the writings, but it continues to maintain the "careful", which we continue to think of the Parliamentary Group of the Galician Nationalist Block which is an indeterminate legal term and which does not require. We therefore argue that the text we presented in Amendment No 13 to the Parliamentary Group on the Nationalist Block in Galicia would be much more appropriate, especially in order to comply with what is precisely laid down in that provision, which is what Articles 4 and 6 of Law 3/1983 and the Statute of Autonomy itself. And, therefore, we believe that it should be noted that in general legal advice his actions, both written and oral, which are developed within the scope of bodies of the Greek administration, public administrations based in Galicia or judicial bodies based in Galicia, will be carried out in the Greek language. It seems to us that it is a much clearer wording, which leaves no doubt that there is a desire to normalize the use of the Greek language in such an important area, Mr. Vice-President, which is also of their competence, I have already told you in the whole debate, such as the Administration of Justice, where only 3% of the judicial resolutions are made in the Greek language and where I am opposed, by personal experience, that the actions of the Galician Council before the judicial organs practically develop not only the oral ones, but also the written ones in Spanish. We also maintain and believe that it would be relevant to amend this law in a positive way, which we have already said was continuous, Amendment No 12, which was intended to add to Article 35, precisely the question of what I was talking about earlier, that specific public summons were periodically established, through the system of competition-opposition, between lawyers of recognized professional experience, both in other public administrations or institutions, as well as in the field of private legal advice and counsel. We believe that this would be a way of enriching Galician Juridical Counsellor in matters that can usually not be so professional. We also maintain Amendment No 11, in relation to the instrumental entities in which we intend to add a new paragraph to Article 33 of the following content: "In addition, in the instrumental entities of the public sector which by its dimension or complexity is considered necessary – for example it might be the case of Sergas or Sogama, or some other, by quoting some – there may be legal advice, and, in any case, a specific legal office will be created for the advice of the entities of the autonomous public sector." And we also keep other amendments that we consider relevant, such as Article #2; Article #3; Article #3; Article #10; Article 32; to quote only a few who have all their lordships and to whom I am therefore not going to read them. And I end by saying that, unfortunately, and despite the good words Mr. Pedro Puy gave us in the whole debate, despite the apparent commitment made by the vice-president in that debate, we believe that the consensus, the desire to reach agreements of the People's Parliamentary Group in the Parliament on matters which could improve this law were so insignificant or non-existent that unfortunately we cannot change the meaning of our vote and that, therefore, we will continue to abstain from this opinion and therefore vote abstaining from this bill. Nothing else and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u68	Open the doors, please. We move on to the second item on the agenda, which corresponds to the full appearance. Comparison of Mr. Health Adviser, on his own request, to introduce the Sergas 2020 Strategy
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u69	That's what the Health Counsellor has to say.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u70	Good morning, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, citizens users and patients are our reason for being. Our work becomes meaningful when we are able to contribute to the maintenance and improvement of their health and quality of life from before birth to the moment it ends. For the Greek Government, the public health system is one of the basic pillars of the welfare state. As such, it must adhere to the basic principles of universality, equity, solidarity and public security, and contribute significantly to redistributive justice and social and territorial cohesion. Its functioning is of great transcendence to each person and also to the whole of society. According to the health barometer, health is for 37% of the Greeks an area of greater interest, only surpassed by work, 40%. As for the opinion on the general functioning of the health system, it highlights the fact that the vast majority of respondents, 73.2%, believe that it is good, or rather good, above the global valuation in Spain, which is 62.7%. Even if the rating is good, we don't comply. Health systems need to be continuously, continuously, tailored to needs and innovative. During the last years, we have introduced relevant innovative improvements in the management, organisation and operation of the health system, with measures such as integrated management, process management, protocolization of priority criteria in the management of waiting lists, deployment of rapid pathways, safety and quality programmes or the promotion of the proper use of pharmacotherapeutic resources, among others. For the present and for the future, we continue to bet on the system by an innovative, proactive, participatory, flexible, sustainable and humanized model. With this philosophy we have developed the work that has led to the Sergas 2020 Strategy. His reason for being can't be anything but to want to improve the health of Greek citizenship. In addition to its content and objectives, it is of the utmost importance that we budget for the public health system. It should be noted that the total estimate for 2020 amounts to more than EUR 17,179 million. We're talking about 40% of the board budget set in Galicia, and we think there's no better evidence of this government's commitment to the public health system. Through this plan we seek to establish innovative strategic guidelines and lines that contribute to addressing the challenges identified in its organization and in its functioning. There are many of the challenges we have on the table: new care realities, increased life expectancy and its consequences, taking on the cost of health services due to the emergence of new and expensive drugs, new technologies, and, above all, we must respond to the needs of an increasingly demanding citizenry. Finally, the health system must be able to adapt to a constantly changing environment and to the new role that patients and citizens play in the interaction with health services. We have much more active citizens, better trained and informed and with greater choice capacity. In most of them they want to be more protagonists in their health, hence the importance of pushing actions aimed at generating more active and expert patients in their own care, given their demand and concerns. Today, the health service user clearly demands that these services meet their needs and want guarantees of comprehensive, ongoing and personalized care. Therefore, we had to be able to definitively involve the patient actively in the definition, design and prioritization of the actions and measures to be implemented for the achievement of these purposes; in short, making our organization a more efficient system in achieving its main goal: raising the health level of Greek citizenship. It's not a minor challenge. Our roadmap to continue the modernization of the health system is its adaptation to the current and future needs of patients and citizens and the Health Plan, made up of two documents: the Priority Plan, whose renewal we presented in September 2014, and the Sergas Strategy, which we now present. It's about the system being able to evolve to respond to a society, as we say, changing. How was the process of developing this Strategy? Clearly, any organization of the complexity of the Galician Health Service needs to have a good definition of its strategy. The first thing I want to explain briefly is how this process of elaboration was. To develop the new planning of Galicia's public health system towards the years 2015-2020, we wanted to count on the perception and opinion of users, as I previously pointed out, but also with professionals and other agents involved in it. We plan it as a process of everyone and for all, together, of accessibility and proximity and listen active to everyone: the patient, the doctor, the locker, the nurse, the manager, the companies in the sector, the students of health professions, the universities. They all count, they're all good. In other words, it was about working with a shared and participatory vision, which I am convinced it was achieved. Initially, we carried out an online process to identify the aspects or topics they considered should be prioritized in the construction of the new Sergas 2020 Strategy. Five group meetings were then held in which students from Medicine and Nursing, the Council and Sergas Advisory Council, members of the Parliamentary Health Commission, trade unions and representatives of technological information and communication companies were invited to attend. The results of the online process have been reviewed in these sessions. It should be noted that 2,248 professionals, 168 patient entities and associations and 644 stakeholders participated in the process as a whole: companies, experts, trade unions, political parties, etc., turning to 2,004 proposals for action, from which he subsequently asked participants to prioritize with a peer-choice system. We have also taken into account an analysis of the strategies of national and international bodies, including the review and incorporation of good practices and interventions that are under way in different countries and health services and existing scientific thinking. We include the use of Murray WHO model on basic goals of any health system: the general goals of the health system, an analysis of complaints and user suggestions, the review of the health barometer, the Strategic Plan of June 2020 and the previous Sergas strategy 2014, and the results of its assessment, presented at its time in this Parliament. We understand that with this procedure we covered several objectives: on the one hand, we managed to generate the shared vision that we had previously commented on; we wanted to identify the priorities of the main stakeholders in the organization, professionals, managers, patients, companies, social partners, etc., to approach a joint vision of the different agents involved in Sergas. On the other hand, establish a line of continuity with the Sergas 2014 strategy, including the results of its evaluation. In addition, we include the current strategic thinking of health systems, as well as the main experiences of the different health services around us. The challenges. From the different approaches and data analyzed at last, we identified the challenges and suggestions that we had to assume for the 2020 Strategy of the Galician Health Service; challenges that we can convert into opportunities. We could group them into ten challenges: The first is to set innovative guidelines and strategic lines that help solve the most important organizational difficulties. The second is to increase the efficiency of the system to assume the increase in the cost of health services for different reasons: more expensive drugs, an increase in population survival, new health technologies and a citizenship with a higher level of demand than demand for increased health investments and which considers that health is a priority in other areas. Third, take good care of the professionals so they can feel part of their organization. It's about valuing these magnificent professions and giving them much more importance to the development of professional excellence. Fourthly, to improve the continuity of care, in addition to encouraging joint work between levels of care or between professionals. Integral care in processes makes patient care possible at a higher level, while improving delays. Fifthly, to improve communication with professionals and with citizenship to achieve the highest credibility of system officials. Sixth, strengthen the standardization and homogeneity of clinical practice to reduce its variability and not compromise the quality of care at any time. Seventh, strengthen organizational and management criteria, so that they are homogeneous and consistent with the objectives of the organization. Eighth, organize services according to the objective needs of patients. Nineteen, develop information systems that allow evaluation and decision-making. And finally, tenth of all, sign resources according to health outcomes. It is necessary to allocate resources according to the needs of patients and to recognize good management also through the redistribution of resources. With all this information and to meet these challenges, the main axes, as well as the associated strategic targets and indicators, were defined. Once these indicators are defined, an analysis of their situation was made to determine the strategic lines needed to achieve the defined targets. From that point on, along with Sergas' management units, the objectives to be achieved in the period 2016-2020, also incorporating those we will continue in the Strategy of 2014. So we've defined four strategic axes and twenty targets that we set ourselves up to 2020. The strategic axes are as follows: integral care adapted to the needs of patients, family members and caregivers; professionals committed and cared for by the organization; axis three, efficient management that contributes to the sustainability of the health system; and fourth, a cross-sectional strategic axis, which is adapting infrastructures to health equipment and information systems to the needs of patients and citizens. These four axes revolve around our main goal: to improve the health of citizens and improve their quality of life. Patients, citizens and users place their trust in us so we can provide them with personalized assistance with the best technology and quality, and in a safe, efficient and effective way. To that end, we count on the involvement, work and commitment of the excellent professionals, organized into structures that facilitate and guarantee their good work. Professionals, in turn, usually approach an individual quality perspective to their clinical practice. His legitimate claim is nothing but to do the right thing precisely. For our band, the Health Administration has a more comprehensive and community-based perspective, requiring adequate technical performance and interpersonal treatment that are satisfactory to patients, and that their consequences be the improvement in the level of health of the community. Strategic lines and projects. Artichokes around these four axes were defined as 20 strategic lines. Without the courage to be exhaustive, I'm going to detail them, because I understand that their importance and transcendence to the health system and to the Greek society in general so justify it. Included in axis 1, integral care adapted to the needs of users, patients, family members and caregivers, we have the following objectives or strategic lines: to develop interventions to promote health and prevent disease with an integral vision; to improve consideration and accessibility in providing services; to guide the provision structure of services to patients' needs; to care for chronicity; to advance patient empowerment; to increase the feeling of ownership and responsibility of citizens over the Galician Health Service; to promote knowledge management, research and improve the reliability of services; to reduce their variability and improve their coordination. In axis 2, focusing on professionals committed and cared for by the organization, we have six objectives, which are: to promote communication with professionals, users and society; to strengthen the credibility of system managers; to strengthen the responsibility and capacity of managers and middle management of the organization; to care for good professionals from when they enter the organization; to enhance the versatility and versatility of professionals in the system; and to involve professionals as a basic criterion in the development and management of the organization. In axis 3, which looks for efficient management that contributes to the sustainability of the health system, we include four objectives: using new resource allocation models to improve the equity of access to services; setting measures to improve the system's efficiency to ensure its sustainability; being a fundamental pillar of Galicia's sustainable development; being a reference to Galicia's social and corporate responsibility; and involving all social agents in projects. Finally, we have axis 4, as we said, a cross-sectional axis, which is to adapt infrastructures to health equipment and information systems to the needs of patients and citizens. We consider it an axis, as I said earlier, transversal, as it facilitates the development of the other three and contemplates three specific objectives: optimizing information systems; prioritizing and ensuring the implementation of technological innovations that have been positively evaluated; and adapting installations and equipment to the needs of users and workers. Thus, these twenty objectives contemplated in the four axes will be achieved through 123 specific projects – some of which I have just mentioned in detail – of which 66 are implemented as new challenges of the organization and the remaining 57 consolidate initiatives launched with the previous Sergas Strategy 2014. So there will be, as otherwise, new things, but, as happens in any solid and consolidated system, the continuity of what has been proved to work well. In the Next Presentation Strategy document you will have access to these projects. In any case, it's about improving to offer all the Greeks, real owners of the public health system, a health service that is increasingly tailored to their needs, with the most advanced technologies and at the same time with the best use of resources. Nothing else at the moment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u123	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen. As you know, the health landscape is evolving, we are facing a different scenario than we had until a very short time ago, in which the system focused primarily on the management of acute diseases. The aging of the population, derived, among other things, from social success and the health system itself, to improve survival, along with the chronicity of diseases, forces us to change. In the Sergas Strategy 2014 we have already taken steps towards managing this new scenario, but we had to continue this evolution. We are working on his assessment with illusion and with the aim of making a better system, reviewing all the actions launched in a critical spirit, in order to strengthen the correct ones and rethink everything that is likely to improve. It was a participatory process. We count on the proximity of internal agents as external. We invite you to participate – as I have pointed out in my first intervention – professionals, users, medical and nursing students, advisory councils, members of the Parliamentary Health and Trade Unions Commission and also representatives of companies. With all these contributions we seek to build a real guide to define where we want to get, where we want the Greek health system to be within five years. This Strategy has the fundamental objective of improving the health of the Greeks through the provision of effective, efficient and quality health services in an equitable, accessible and sustainable way, consolidating both technological innovation and the implementation of integrated processes that guarantee their care continuity. I want to summarize again the fundamentals of the Strategy that we present today: that our mission is to improve the health of the Greeks. To continue progressing toward this goal, we have identified four strategic axes or targets that will help improve the health of the Greeks. They're the ones I detailed in my first intervention. Once those axes are defined, you will need to need the different strategic lines that would develop them. These lines may in many cases be contributing to developing more than one strategic axis, but by ordering the document, each strategic line assigning it to the axis where it might most impact. Once the lines are set, the projects have been defined as operational tools for the development of the Strategy. As normal, each project can be related to several strategic lines and therefore to several axes. For the order of the plan, each project has been assigned to the strategic axis that best adapts to a strategic line that develops mainly. The final summary of this structuring reflects that in an organization as complex as Sergas all projects interact with the different strategic objectives and thus contribute to developing several strategic lines, although in some countries it always has a greater impact, and so the list of projects was ordered. With these projects we want to continue the changes that the system needs to make stronger, to overcome the challenges it must undoubtedly face: the ageing of the population, its pluripathology, technology and increasingly expensive drugs, adaptation to new information and communication technologies, more and more informed patients, increasingly involved in the decision on their health and disease, the inevitable needs of health prevention and promotion and increasingly transformative actions in the strict healthcare field and the new rules of play with health providers. Also within these lines of guiding principles of the Galego Health Service, a system that thinks about citizenship above all, we have to focus on the accessibility and fairness of health benefits for the entire population of Galicia. To achieve this goal, we need to be able to continue the process of adapting to new information and communication technologies in order to establish relationships of true proximity between the levels of care and to achieve a relationship between the physician and the patient of a new medicine that is constantly evolving. The strategy also aims to enhance good professionals and to influence the development of professional excellence. We want to continue to improve care continuity by promoting joint work among professional levels. We aspire to standardize clinical practice to reduce its variability and personalize services according to citizens' objective needs. In this sense, the Strategy advocates adapting care flows to the different types of patients, as the system is now organized by medical specialities and professional categories, not by the needs of users. Finally, the initiative seeks to achieve the utmost credibility of system officials to improve the communication of professionals and citizens, in favour of the implementation of new projects, strengthen organizational and management criteria that are homogeneous and coherent, develop information systems that allow evaluation and decision-making and allocate resources based on health outcomes. Finally, we will continue to work on the integration of care, in the structure itself, in the implementation of action plans at both levels of assistance, using all the tools that we have already set up and which facilitate activity in the presence, communication, and which ultimately enhance the resolution capacity of primary care. Part of these performances are going to be done with the development of programs that will use digital home and hospital at home, where the patient also has a relevant role. We want to conjugate the technician, the specialization and the human. That's why we can't forget a very important aspect: the relationship with patient associations. Their participation is a clearly strategic objective. We want to incorporate his voice, his vision and his worries; we aspire to be an open health service to them, to continue to encourage his participation and to open hospitals and health centers to society. The intention is to respond and support a growing number of patient associations, whose role in relation to our organization changes with the passage of time, acquiring larger dimensions every day. We want to incorporate them decisively and definitively and get more trained and informed patients, more experienced and competent patients in their self-care. It is necessary to renew the dynamics of the functioning of the Advisory Council for Patients. At the very least, our goal is to maintain its current activity as an advisory body of the Council and continue to support its training demands, as well as institutionally support its progress and official actions. In essence and summary, and given the sociodemographic characteristics of our community, with an accused population aging, we are committed to an adequate system of attention to chronic diseases. The importance of attention to chronic patients, both because of their incidence and their impact on the system, determines the needs of a reengineering in their assistance. The basis of the same is also established in primary care and should achieve the highest possible efficiency, avoiding duplication of tests and diagnostic and therapeutic viability and preventing hospital income. To that end, it will be necessary to develop an care philosophy based on integrated care processes and a different work dynamics among professionals, where you have a more comprehensive view of their complexity and their environment, anticipating your needs and punctual actions in acute phases, without forgetting the legitimate wishes of the patient. It also requires the application of the new management formulas, in which the patient should be incorporated, providing him with knowledge and skills that enable him to participate and co-responsibility of health decisions. We're going well forward, we're still moving forward, though there's a lot left to go on. There's an African proverb that says if you want to walk fast alone, but if you want to get away walk accompanied. That's why I want to end by thanking all the professionals of the Galician Health Service and the Health Council, my team – who is here today – and the best incentive and incentive to continue working for the improvement of the health and quality of life of the Greeks and the Greeks. And, finally, because it remained in the debate that Galicia is left behind in health care with regard to Europe, I would like Members to be able to travel and talk about German health, Portuguese health or the health of those countries that are said to be above us. They pay for services, and a German has to pay every day for hospital admission, a Portuguese paid for emergencies and medical care; that Portuguese had a problem: he had a government that couldn't cut the economic crisis and had to be intervened by Europe. And I was telling Mrs. Prado that there was not a government that had done as much damage to health as the current one. I think there were several. She participated in the Bipartite here in Galicia, but we all suffer the situation that caused Mr. Zapatero in Spain and that today we are still paying. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u206	The opposition against Royal Decree 954/2015 of 23 October, which regulates – or better prohibits – the indication, use, use and dispensation of medicinal products and medical products for human use by nurses and nurses, is only serving to create a war between medical and nursing schools. The acceptance of Article 3.2, as drafted, implies that at present the nursing staff would have to stop performing many of the functions they perform autonomously, and it is not feasible today to assume these functions by the medical staff. Apart from not being assumed by medical personnel according to their own ratios and workload, it's completely illogical to use medical personnel, to hire medical personnel, to perform functions that really throughout Europe are performing nursing staff. Therefore, it is essential to take the necessary measures to guarantee legal security, since, by treating patients as they do, they end up having legal security problems because now the law prevents them from caring for these patients, and Sergas does nothing. Thus, the Spanish General Nursing Council's study office, in its Report on Health Human Resources in Spain and the European Union 2014, collected that in Europe there are 29.06 % doctors, compared to 70.94 % nursing staff; a distribution of 70-30 – 70 nursing staff, 30 medical staff – which is considered efficient for the study's authors. On average, in the European Union of 28, there are ratios of 811 nurses or nurses and 322 doctors per 100,000 inhabitants. In Galicia the ratios are 501 nurses – i.e. 300 nurses less per 100,000 inhabitants than in Europe – and 425 doctors – i.e. 100 physicians more average than in Europe. We're hiring optional staff to do nursing work, and therefore it's a real absurd policy. It seems obvious that nursing staff can take on a lot more work, a lot more responsibilities, with their training because they are doing so in Europe. In the Spanish state, however, it seems that the purpose of denigrating the nursing profession to a completely medical-dependent sub-professional profession is to denigrate. Because health is much more than medicine. Health is much more than surgery. Health involves daily care to the patient, also psychological care to the patient and also attention to the families who accompany these patients, something that does not do the medical staff, that passes a view that lasts 5 or 10 minutes, at most, but the rest of the day, when they are in the hospital, are cared for by nursing staff, who really knows the patient, their families and the problems involved in it. It is essential to put your feet on the ground and not start with these nonsense of decrees, these crazy lies that you're lying about the health reality, a reality that exists only for you, and to accommodate this debate to society as a whole; society's participation is necessary to achieve the health system that we want. And besides with a nursing staff who, if they really recognize the functions that they can perform according to their training, we would have a medical staff longer to care for the patients, more time to investigate...

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u214	Good afternoon, everyone. Today we bring here the debate the prescription of nursing. The real decree as stated here throughout the world is born with an end: to develop and improve the professional expectations of a health sector as important and fundamental as that of nursing in the health system, both in primary and specialized care. We must remember that in Spain today they work about 270,000 professionals. It must be shown that there has not been a healthy branch that has experienced similar growth over the last forty years. Since the formation, which lacked university rank in the 1970s, it was sufficient to own the 4th bachelor's degree, the extinct bachelor's degree, when the professionals were called practitioners and ATS. Until the early 1980s, no new nursing profession, with a degree of university diplomacy, appeared, three years of theoretical and practical study to which there was already access with COU and selectivity, as to any other university career. Currently they are no longer graduates, they are graduates, they already require four years of training, and from there they can access specialized training through the EIR system, similar to that carried out with doctors, pharmacists, biologists, chemists or psychologists. It is clear that the improvement of training and the acquisition of new knowledge involves new competences. The Galician Health Service has increased nursing skills. I didn't see anyone here talking about today that nursing professionals can make analytical requests so they can continue to control patients with chronic processes such as diabetes, hypertension, hypercholesterolemia, obesity. They can also make microbiological petitions when they affect the cures they were carrying out. You don't have to ask your doctor for permission to make an analytical request. In 2006, the modification of the drug law allowed dentists and podologists to prescribe medicinal products and medical products for human use. Until that date, prescription was solely the competence of medical professionals. In this law the prescription of nursing was relegated, it took nine years for the Royal Decree 954/2015 to appear, which will allow the prescription. This decree, which entered into force at the end of December 2015, consists of three chapters, eleven articles and several additional, transitional and final provisions. It has two aspects that cause controversy in different groups, both are in Article 3.2. The first is the need to get an accreditation after a 180-hour course. And the second thing is, prior to prescription, there has to be a diagnosis, an indication and a protocol. I want to show that the majority of Sergas' staff in all their categories, from optional staff to cell, are governed by Law 55/2003, known as the Framework Statute, which governs in all communities and regulates the functions that correspond to each category; that is, the functions of a doctor, nursing, administrative, auxiliaries and janitors. It would be interesting if a lot of people had read the royal decree, because that way they could know what their functions are, especially when we talk about medical staff and nursing staff. This may condition that the royal decree has collided with this law, because in that law it clearly specifies what the functions of the doctor and the nurse are. To finish, I wanted to remind you that this royal decree was travelled by the SATSE and the, pretending to be the cautious suspension of Article 3.2. The Supreme Court has now denied this precautionary suspension, and today we have been aware that it has also rejected an appeal by the Balearic Government to stop applying the Royal Decree of Nursing, according to the digital press. Thank you.
