Popular of Galicia, at the initiative of D José Manuel Balsairo Orol, on the demands to be made by the Government of Greece to the Central Government for the improvement of the system of contribution and socio-laboral protection of people working at sea
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u245	This initiative will be presented with two amendments, one from the Joint Group and one from the Nationalist Block Group in Galicia.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u246	To formulate the non-law proposition Mr. Balseiro Orol has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-03-29-DSPG146.u247	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are moving to this House an initiative that we believe will be beneficial to all seafarers. It emerges, not the exclusive impulse of this parliamentary group, of the People's Parliamentary Group, but as an idea outlined by the professionals themselves of the maritime-fishing confrries at the regular meetings that I had the honour to maintain in recent times. In our view and in the opinion of the People's Parliamentary Group, this initiative will result in greater social and work protection present and future for professionals at sea. And it's born after a specific context. The Government of the People's Party in Spain has promoted legislation that has just completed a significant change and a positive change in regulations regulating the social regime of men and women working in the marine-fishing sector. We speak, ladies and gentlemen, of the Law 45/2015 of 21 October; a legal text that introduces important changes, among others, the extension of the collectives covered by the protective mechanisms provided for in the special social security regime for the workers of the sea. We will all agree that the particular characteristics, hardness, seasonality or risks inherent in the activity of the sea always require exclusive protection regulation. By overcoming this first milestone, we understand from the People's Parliamentary Group that there are areas for further progress in improving professionals, improving the economic and social wellbeing of these workers. One of these areas that is likely to improve is the social security contribution scheme. Between the vast majority of workers in Spain when they meet the requirements for access to a pension they get some accounts that do not differ substantially from those they perceive while they are active, it does not happen the same with all the professionals invited to the special regime of sea workers, for example, those whose work is paid by the traditional system of the part, and the rest of those who are confined, in this case to groups of quote 2 and 3, among those who are self-employed from the sea. They would not be paying for their real wages in this case, but for legal estimates. This situation is detrimental to these workers and their future pensions because they are pensions in these lower cases. That's why, as I've told you before, there are multiple reasons why this discrepancy is motivated. Without attempting to get into technical aspects, they are based on the different assumptions that the current legislation establishes to determine the worker's income. Today the law regulates a mechanism too artificial that, as a final result, offers a lot of counts under reality or, in the case of the autonomous of the sea, tied by an equal fixed quota for all of them and a quota, in this case, that is unremovable. Well, that's the sense of our initiative. Our initiative is born to correct the problem we just described. Ladies and gentlemen, you know very well that the special scheme of self-employed people has allowed since its creation the possibility of choosing a basis of quote according to their economic possibilities and expectations or also profit expectations, always within limits established according to the worker's age. This same advantage is also provided for agricultural workers on their own. In the People's Party we believe that women and men in the sea also deserve this same treatment of what we have just talked about, that professionals in the sea deserve the opportunity to benefit from the advantages that would imply a similar model. The People's Parliamentary Group therefore calls on the administrations to promote regulatory reform in terms and with the limitations which could be agreed upon in the process of dialogue with professionals in the maritime-fishing sector which would enable a similar system to be put in place for the free choice of the different basis of contributions, allowing voluntary increases – and I repeat voluntarily – above the established minimum base. From a practical point of view, a reform of these characteristics would be very beneficial for all professionals in the world of the sea in our autonomous community, which, as a result, would have improved their future pensions. And so since the People's Parliamentary Group we urge the Council of Galicia to address the Central Government to continue its management and to push for policy changes that are needed to improve the system of contribution and social and labour protection for people working at sea. We call on the various administrations to study, among other things, the possibilities of establishing a system that will enable them to voluntarily increase their contribution bases, and whenever they do not assume loss or injury to these workers. In short, ladies and gentlemen, we call for the implementation of a voluntary system of improvement in the contributions that professionals of the sea make to Social Security. Finally, we look for ways to improve the social protection of our sailors and our workers in the Galician Sea.
